| --- |

&#x20;In the opening minutes of the first quarter, [Donte DiVincenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donte_DiVincenzo) suffered an [Achilles tendon rupture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achilles_tendon_rupture) following an awkward landing, ending his season and playoff run. To make matters worse for Minnesota, [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\)) suffered a hyperextended knee late in the second quarter and would not return. Denver took advantage of Edwards' absence, leading 54–50 at halftime. However, in what ABC announcer [Mike Breen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Breen) referred to as the "[Ayo Dosunmu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayo_Dosunmu) Game", Dosunmu came off the bench and scored 43 points, just the fourth time in NBA playoff history that a player ever scored 40-plus off the bench. He finished the night with spectacular efficiency, on 13-of-17 shooting from the field, and a perfect 5 of 5 from 3-point range and 12 of 12 from the free throw line. Including Dosunmu, the Timberwolves' bench outscored the Nuggets' a whopping 76–16, as the former forced 9 of the latter's 10 turnovers in the second half to swing the game in their favor. [Jamal Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamal_Murray) led Denver with 30 points, whereas [Nikola Jokić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Joki%C4%87) finished with 24 points, but shot 8 of 22 from the field and committed 4 turnovers despite a near triple-double. This would mark the Nuggets' third straight loss for just the second time all season.

With 1.2 seconds remaining, [Jaden McDaniels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaden_McDaniels) ran down the court and scored a layup, after which Jokić physically attacked him for breaking an [unwritten rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unspoken_rule) that the winning team should not score during the final seconds of a game when the outcome has already been decided. This immediately sparked an altercation between both teams' benches, resulting in Jokić and [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) being ejected from the game.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164]

The game, which originally aired on ABC, was moved to [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN) in the second half after live breaking news coverage of the [2026 White House Correspondents' Dinner shooting](83038072.md).[^cite_note-165]

[Nikola Jokić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Joki%C4%87) had a [triple-double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-double) and [Spencer Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spencer_Jones_\(basketball\)) provided a key spark, with the Nuggets staving off elimination in a 125–113 win at the [Ball Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball_Arena). Jokić's full stat line was 27 points, 16 assists and 12 rebounds, good enough for his 23rd career triple-double. The Timberwolves, who are short-handed for the remainder of the series without [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\)) and [Donte DiVincenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donte_DiVincenzo), trailed by as much as 27 points before cutting the margin to 10 in the fourth quarter. [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) led Minnesota with 27 points and [Ayo Dosunmu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayo_Dosunmu) had 18. [Naz Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naz_Reid) twisted his ankle but returned later in the game.[^cite_note-166]

The Timberwolves eliminated the Nuggets for the second time in three years with a Game 6 victory at home. With [Ayo Dosunmu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayo_Dosunmu) (calf soreness) joining [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\)) and [Donte DiVincenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donte_DiVincenzo) on the inactive list, Minnesota went big with [Rudy Gobert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Gobert), [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) and [Naz Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naz_Reid) fueling a 64–40 advantage in points in the paint and a 50–33 edge in rebounding. [Jaden McDaniels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaden_McDaniels) led the Wolves with 32 points and 10 rebounds and [Terrence Shannon Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrence_Shannon_Jr.) scored 24 points in a rare start. [Nikola Jokić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Joki%C4%87) had 28 points, 10 assists and nine rebounds to lead the Nuggets, but [Jamal Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamal_Murray) struggled and finished with just 12 points on 4-for-17 shooting. [Cameron Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Johnson) scored 27 points for Denver.[^cite_note-167]

| [Crypto.com Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com_Arena), [Los Angeles](18110.md), CA **Attendance:** 19,057 **Referees:** James Capers, [Tyler Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyler_Ford_\(basketball\)), Ed Malloy |

&#x20;In a game with stars [Kevin Durant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Durant), [Luka Dončić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Don%C4%8Di%C4%87), and [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) all sidelined due to injury, the Lakers were able to win the short-handed duel 107–98, which marked their first Game 1 victory in the playoffs since the [2023 Western Conference Semifinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_NBA_playoffs#\(6\)_Golden_State_Warriors_vs._\(7\)_Los_Angeles_Lakers). Los Angeles never trailed since early in the 1st quarter, where [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) dished out eight assists in the quarter alone and ten in a half (both career highs), finishing with 13 on the night. [Luke Kennard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Kennard) led the Lakers in scoring with 27 points, whereas [Deandre Ayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deandre_Ayton) notched a double-double. For Houston, [Alperen Şengün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alperen_%C5%9Eeng%C3%BCn) led the team with 19 points but shot just 6 of 19 from the field. The entire Rockets team struggled shooting as a whole, on just 38 percent (35 of 93) from the field and 33 percent from 3-point range, while the Lakers' offense flourished, shooting 61 percent from the field and a remarkable 53 percent from three despite 18 turnovers.[^cite_note-169]

[Kevin Durant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Durant)'s return from injury proved inconsequential for the Rockets as they once again experienced defeat. [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) led the way for the victorious Lakers with 28 points, 8 rebounds and 7 assists. [Marcus Smart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Smart) and [Luke Kennard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Kennard) added 25 and 23 points, respectively. For Houston, Durant scored 23 points, while teammate [Alperen Şengün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alperen_%C5%9Eeng%C3%BCn) logged a double-double with 20 points and 11 rebounds, along with 5 assists, 4 steals, and 2 blocks. As a team, the Lakers were 13-for-28 on 3-pointers overall, while the Rockets went 7-for-29.[^cite_note-170]

| [Toyota Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Center), [Houston](13774.md), TX **Attendance:** 18,055 **Referees:** [Zach Zarba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zach_Zarba), Ray Acosta, Tre Maddox |
| --- |

&#x20;Prior to the game, it was announced that [Kevin Durant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Durant) would sit out his second game of the series after an ankle sprain suffered in Game 2. Even without [Luka Dončić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Don%C4%8Di%C4%87) and [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) for the third straight game, the Lakers started hot with an 11-point lead at halftime, and at one point a 15-point lead in the 2nd half. Even so, the Rockets managed to claw their way back to take the lead, holding a 6-point advantage with 31 seconds remaining. However, after a costly foul on a 3-point shot by [Jae'Sean Tate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jae'Sean_Tate) on [Marcus Smart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Smart) and a turnover which led to a game-tying 3-pointer by [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) near the end of regulation, the game went into overtime following a missed shot by [Alperen Şengün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alperen_%C5%9Eeng%C3%BCn) and a 3-pointer by James that rimmed out. Los Angeles took control in overtime, becoming the first team in the playoffs to take a commanding 3–0 series lead, while Houston lost their 3rd consecutive game for just the 2nd time all season long. Despite eight turnovers, James led the Lakers with 29 points, 13 rebounds, and 6 assists, while Smart added 21 points and 10 assists. For the Rockets, Şengün scored a game-high 33 points and had 16 rebounds, whereas [Amen Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amen_Thompson) had 26 points and 11 rebounds. However, the bench output was the difference maker between both teams, with Los Angeles' bench outscoring Houston's 24–3.[^cite_note-171]

After a heartbreaking overtime loss in Game 3, the Rockets avoided a series sweep with a blowout win over the Lakers. Still in the absence of star player [Kevin Durant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Durant), [Amen Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amen_Thompson) led his team with 23 points. All of the Houston starters reached double-figures; [Tari Eason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tari_Eason) scored 20, [Alperen Şengün](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alperen_%C5%9Eeng%C3%BCn) scored 19, [Reed Sheppard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_Sheppard) scored 17, and [Jabari Smith Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jabari_Smith_Jr.) scored 16. [Deandre Ayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deandre_Ayton) led Los Angeles with 19 points and 10 rebounds but was ejected with over 5 minutes remaining in the third quarter due to a flagrant foul on Şengün.[^cite_note-172] [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) was also held to 10 points throughout this game.

| --- |

A balanced team effort helped the Rockets continue their postseason, as they defeated the Lakers on the road. Each one of Houston's starters was in double figures in scoring, with [Jabari Smith Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jabari_Smith_Jr.) leading the way at 22 points. [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) played in his first game in nearly a month and scored 22 points but shot 4 of 16 from the field. He was involved in a crucial play late where he originally took a charge foul by [Tari Eason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tari_Eason), but the Rockets challenged the call, and the referees determined Reaves was not in legal guarding position, thus calling it a blocking foul instead. At that point, the Lakers were in a late surge to try to take advantage of another Houston collapse, similar to Game 3. However, [Reed Sheppard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_Sheppard) hit a mid-range jump shot and stripped [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) of the ball in the final minutes to give the Rockets a comfortable lead. James scored 17 of his 25 points in the second half. He lost a closeout game at home for the first time since Game 5 of the first-round series against the [Washington Wizards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%E2%80%9308_Washington_Wizards_season) in [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_NBA_playoffs). Additionally, the Lakers held an all-time franchise record of 40–1 when leading a playoff series 3–1. Their only previous loss before this game in that situation came back in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_NBA_playoffs) against the [Phoenix Suns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_Phoenix_Suns_season), losing three straight games after [Kobe Bryant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobe_Bryant)'s overtime game-winner in Game 4.[^cite_note-173]

The Lakers finished off the Rockets in Game 6 behind a 28-point effort from [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James). They used a dominant 27–3 run in parts of the first and second quarter to lead by 18 points at halftime. In the third quarter, they led by as much as 22 points. [Rui Hachimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rui_Hachimura) added 21 points with five 3-pointers and [Deandre Ayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deandre_Ayton) pulled down 16 rebounds. The Rockets shot poorly throughout the game, shooting just 35% from the field and making just 5-of-28 3-pointers; in particular, [Reed Sheppard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_Sheppard) went 1-for-10 from three. This is the Lakers' first time advancing to the West Semifinals since [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_Los_Angeles_Lakers_season).[^cite_note-174]

With the Game 1 victory, Detroit ended a record-tying 12-game postseason losing streak against Cleveland, a drought that dated back to the [2007 Eastern Conference Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_NBA_playoffs#\(1\)_Detroit_Pistons_vs._\(2\)_Cleveland_Cavaliers). [Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) scored 23 points, [Tobias Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tobias_Harris) had 20, [Duncan Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duncan_Robinson) added 19 points on 5-of-8 from three, and [Jalen Duren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Duren) pulled down 12 rebounds. [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell) led the Cavaliers in scoring with 23 points. Like in the first round against Toronto, [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) struggled with turning the ball over with 7 in the game (the Cavs had 20 turnovers overall as a team), to go along with 22 points. Cleveland big man [Jarrett Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarrett_Allen) had just 2 points and 3 rebounds after a 22-point, 19-rebound performance in Game 7 of the first round against the Raptors.[^cite_note-176]

[Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) had 25 points and 10 assists and [Tobias Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tobias_Harris) scored 21 points, as the Pistons defended their home-court advantage to take a 2–0 series lead in Game 2. They have won five straight games since being down [3–1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_teams_that_have_overcome_3%E2%80%931_series_deficits) to the Magic in the first round. [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell) scored 31 points, leading the Cavaliers, and [Jarrett Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarrett_Allen) rebounded from his poor Game 1 with 22 points and 7 rebounds. However, [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) struggled, missing 10-of-13 shots and committing four turnovers, including one that came when the Cavs were down six with 33 seconds. Thus far in the series, Harden has more turnovers (11) than field goal makes (10).[^cite_note-177]

The Cavaliers stayed perfect in the 2026 playoffs at home with a 116–109 win in Game 2. [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell) scored 35 points on 13-of-24 shooting and 10 rebounds, and [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) added 19 points on 8-of-14 shooting, which included multiple clutch baskets in the closing minutes of the game to seal the win. Cleveland blew a 17-point third quarter lead, but recovered in the fourth quarter, outscoring Detroit 33–28. [Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) led the Pistons with 27 points and 10 assists but had 8 turnovers. For the eighth consecutive game, [Tobias Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tobias_Harris) scored 20-points or more, as he finished with 21 points on 7-of-14 shooting.[^cite_note-178]

The Cavaliers turned a four-point halftime deficit into a dominant victory behind a historic performance from [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell). Mitchell scored 39 of his 43 points in the second half and spearheaded a 24–0 run early in the second half. [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) had a double-double of 24 points and 11 assists, while [Evan Mobley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evan_Mobley) had 17 points. Mitchell tied [Sleepy Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleepy_Floyd) ([1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_NBA_playoffs)) for the most second half points scored in a playoff game. While neither starter on the Pistons side scored more than 20 points, [Caris LeVert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caris_LeVert) came off the bench and led with 24 points, while also holding off [Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) under 20 points for the first time this season.[^cite_note-Collier-30]

| --- |

The Cavaliers stunned the Pistons to erase a 103–94 with two-plus minutes left, and win 117–113 in overtime, taking a 3–2 series lead back home. Detroit also led by 15-points at various moments in the first half, but they went cold at the end of the game, with Cleveland on a 13–0 run for a five-minute stretch in between the fourth quarter and overtime. [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) led the Cavs with 30 points in his best playoff game with the team in his first season. There was some controversy at the end of regulation with the score tied when [Jarrett Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarrett_Allen) seemingly tripped [Ausar Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausar_Thompson) on a scramble to a loose ball, but no call was made.[^cite_note-179] [Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) had 39 points and 9 assists, but 6 turnovers. Detroit will look to improve to 4–0 thus far this playoffs in elimination games after winning three straight against the [Orlando Magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Orlando_Magic_season) in the first round.[^cite_note-180]

| --- |

The Pistons staved off elimination with a balanced effort in a 115–94 road win against the Cavaliers. [Cade Cunningham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cade_Cunningham) led Detroit with 21 points, but had more of a struggle with his efficiency, only shooting 7-of-19 and turning the ball over 7 times. He was picked up by his teammates such as [Jalen Duren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Duren), who broke out of his slump this playoffs, with 15 points and 11 rebounds. [Daniss Jenkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniss_Jenkins) also had 15 points, and [Paul Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Reed_\(basketball\)) chipped in with 17 while [Duncan Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duncan_Robinson) had 14 off the bench. [Ausar Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausar_Thompson) was effective on both sides of the ball with 10 points, 9 rebounds, and 4 steals. For Cleveland, [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell) scored 18 points on 6-of-20 shooting while being guarded mostly by Thompson. [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) had 23 points, but for much of the playoffs so far, struggled holding on to the ball, as he had 8 turnovers. This is the Cavaliers' and Pistons' second Game 7 this playoffs, as both played in one in the first round. Cleveland lost their first home game of this playoffs, falling to 6–1.[^cite_note-181]

Further information: [List of NBA game sevens](15664762.md)

Entering the game, the Pistons were 4–0 when facing elimination this postseason, while the Cavaliers have posted a road record of 1–5.

Cleveland completed the upset over top-seeded Detroit with a dominant 31-point victory. The Cavaliers reached their first Eastern Conference Finals appearance without [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) on the roster since [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_NBA_playoffs), and first overall since [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NBA_playoffs). In their 6 previous appearances, they were 5–1, including four consecutive finals wins from 2015–2018, with their only loss in [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_NBA_playoffs) to the [Orlando Magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_Orlando_Magic_season). With the win, star guard [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell), who had 26 points and 8 rebounds in the game, made his first conference finals in his ninth season. This was the Pistons' first Game 7 loss at home, going 5–0 in their previous such games.[^cite_note-182]

&#x20;The Knicks decimated the 76ers in Game 1, as [Jalen Brunson](43132078.md) scored 27 of his 35 points in the 1st half to give New York a 23-point halftime lead. The game was never close since the 1st quarter, with New York outscoring Philadelphia 104–73 since. With this victory, it marked the first time in NBA history a playoff team had won 3 consecutive playoff games by 25 points or more, with the Knicks having won their games by 29, 51 and 39 points respectively, nearly 40 points on average. In the 76ers' first playoff road loss since Game 1 against the [Boston Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025-26_Boston_Celtics_season) in round one, [Paul George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_George) led the team with 17 points, with [Joel Embiid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Embiid) and [Tyrese Maxey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrese_Maxey) shooting a combined 6 of 20 from the field, despite both finishing in double figures. Backup center [Adem Bona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adem_Bona) notably recorded 5 fouls and 3 turnovers in 4 minutes of play, going scoreless in this span.[^cite_note-184]

| ***New York leads series, 2–0*** |  |  |

| [Madison Square Garden](75129.md), [New York](645042.md), NY **Attendance:** 19,812 **Referees:** [Tony Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Brothers), [Tyler Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyler_Ford_\(basketball\)), Brian Forte |
| --- |

The Knicks won a hard-fought Game 2 to take a commanding 2–0 series lead. The contest featured 25 lead changes, which was the most in a playoff game in 11 years, and 14 ties. Neither the 76ers nor Knicks led by more than seven points the entire 60 minutes. Prior to the game [Joel Embiid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Embiid) was ruled out with a right hip and ankle injury. Guard [Tyrese Maxey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrese_Maxey) stepped up scoring 26 points for Philadelphia, but was matched by [Jalen Brunson](43132078.md), who also scored 26, and made multiple clutch baskets down the stretch for New York. [OG Anunoby](52672338.md) added 24 points and [Karl-Anthony Towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl-Anthony_Towns) had 20 points, 10 rebounds and 7 assists. Anunoby left the game late, seemingly with a hamstring injury, and did not return. Due to the absence of star Embiid, Maxey and [Paul George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_George) played 46 and 42 minutes, respectively, and struggled shooting the ball in the fourth quarter.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186]

| ***New York leads series, 3–0*** |  |  |

| [Xfinity Mobile Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Mobile_Arena), [Philadelphia](50585.md), PA **Attendance:** 19,746 **Referees:** Marc Davis, Josh Tiven, Ray Acosta |
| --- |

&#x20;After an early 12-point lead for Philadelphia, New York slowly clawed their way back into the game and captured the lead in the second quarter, never seizing it and taking a 3–0 series lead, which also marked their sixth straight playoff victory, and seventh consecutive victory at [Xfinity Mobile Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Mobile_Arena) dating back to the [2024 NBA playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NBA_playoffs). Despite [OG Anunoby](52672338.md) being ruled out for the game due to a hamstring injury, the Knicks never wavered in his absence, with [Jalen Brunson](43132078.md) having a spectacular performance once again, finishing with 33 points, 5 rebounds and 9 assists, and [Mikal Bridges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikal_Bridges) added 23 points in a dominant win for New York. The 76ers were out-rebounded 49–33 despite a rough shooting night from beyond the arc for the Knicks (33%, 9–27), with [Tyrese Maxey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrese_Maxey) playing 44 minutes but only scoring 17 points, and [Paul George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_George) finished with all 15 of his points in the first quarter, going 0–9 since.[^cite_note-187]

| ***New York wins series, 4–0*** |  |  |

| [Xfinity Mobile Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Mobile_Arena), [Philadelphia](50585.md), PA **Attendance:** 19,746 **Referees:** John Goble, Sean Wright, [Nick Buchert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Buchert) |
| --- |

&#x20;In a dominant effort, the Knicks completed just the third ever playoff series sweep in franchise history in emphatic fashion, while clinching their second straight Eastern Conference Finals berth. [Miles McBride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miles_McBride) started off the game 4 of 4 from 3-point range, ultimately finishing with 25 points and 7 made 3-pointers, a team high for New York. In the opening quarter, the Knicks made 11 3-pointers and built up a 19-point lead at the end of the quarter, with the former being an NBA playoff record. They ultimately tied the NBA playoff record for most 3-pointers made in a playoff game with 25, while taking their 2nd 30-plus point victory over Philadelphia in the series, and extended their postseason winning streak to 7 games. This also marked the 8th straight win for the Knicks over the 76ers at [Xfinity Mobile Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xfinity_Mobile_Arena). In a "home game" for the Knicks due to the massive number of New York fans, cheers heard from Philadelphia fans were extremely limited, and a dismal team performance facing a sweep did not make things any better. [Joel Embiid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joel_Embiid) scored 24 points on 8 of 8 shooting from the field, but his teammates failed to deliver, with [Paul George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_George), [V. J. Edgecombe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._J._Edgecombe) and [Tyrese Maxey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrese_Maxey) scoring a combined 32 points on 13 of 37 shooting. In addition, the Knicks dominated the glass 47–30, with New York having more defensive rebounds (32) than Philadelphia had collectively as a team (30).[^cite_note-188]

[Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) led the way with 24 points and 12 rebounds, as the Thunder stayed undefeated in the 2026 postseason in Game 1. The Lakers stayed close for most of the game, but Oklahoma City pulled ahead by double-digits thanks to an end of the third quarter surge. Playing the #1 defense in the league, Los Angeles struggled without [Luka Dončić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Don%C4%8Di%C4%87), shooting 41% from the field and 33% from three. Most notably, [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) was 3-of-16 and scored just 8 points, which was the fewest makes ever by a Lakers' player in the playoffs with 15 or more shot attempts.[^cite_note-190]

| [Paycom Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paycom_Center), [Oklahoma City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oklahoma_City), OK **Attendance:** 18,203 **Referees:** John Goble, Ben Taylor, Mark Lindsay |

With star [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) in foul trouble early, [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) (22 points), [Ajay Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajay_Mitchell) (20), and [Jared McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jared_McCain) (18 points, 4-of-5 from three) stepped up as the Thunder took a 2–0 series with a 125–107 win in Game 2. Gilgeous-Alexander still tied Holmgren to lead his team in scoring but picked up four fouls early in the third quarter and was double-teamed all game, forcing open looks for his teammates. The game was similar to Game 1, in that the Lakers stuck with the Thunder for much of the game (even leading by five in the third quarter), until Oklahoma City surged ahead and never surrendered the lead from the middle of the third quarter onwards. [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) had his best game thus far in the playoffs, leading Los Angeles with a playoff-career-high 31 points, though he had 5 turnovers against 6 assists. [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) added 23 and [Rui Hachimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rui_Hachimura) had 16. The game was chippy throughout, leading to a small confrontation between Reaves and referee John Goble after the game and prompting head coach [JJ Redick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JJ_Redick) to criticize the officials in the post-game press conference.[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192]

[Ajay Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajay_Mitchell) continued to stellar series in place of injured forward [Jalen Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Williams), scoring a playoff career-high 24 points and dishing out 10 assists, as the Thunder took a commanding 3–0 series lead in Game 3 in Los Angeles. [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) added 23 points and 9 assists and [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) scored 18 points and had 9 rebounds for Oklahoma City, who moved to 7–0 in the playoffs. Game 3 was similar to the first two games as Los Angeles made it a game for a half. However, the Thunder outscored the Lakers in the third quarter 33–20 and continued with a 41-point fourth quarter. [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) and [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) combined to shoot 12 for 32 from the field. [Rui Hachimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rui_Hachimura) led the Lakers with 21 points.[^cite_note-193]

| [Crypto.com Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com_Arena), [Los Angeles](18110.md), CA **Attendance:** 19,057 **Referees:** [Tony Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Brothers), Kevin Scott, Justin Van Duyne |
| --- |

The Thunder capped off their undefeated record versus the Lakers in both the regular season and the playoffs with a 115–110 win to advanced to their second straight Conference Finals. Los Angeles trailed by 4 points at halftime and led by 4 points entering the 4th quarter. However, they were outscored 26–35 in the final quarter. [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) made a go-ahead dunk to break the 110–110 tie with 32.5 seconds remaining. Oklahoma City were led by [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) who scored 35 points. [Ajay Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajay_Mitchell) scored a playoff career-high 28 points, 10 of which were in the 4th quarter. In a losing effort, [Austin Reaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Reaves) scored 27 points but missed a game-tying three-pointer in the closing seconds as the Lakers were swept out of the playoffs for the first time since the [2023 Western Conference Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_NBA_playoffs#Western_Conference_finals) against the eventual champion [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_Denver_Nuggets_season). [Rui Hachimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rui_Hachimura) scored 25 points, including a key [four-point play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-point_play) in the closing minutes, and [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James) had 24 points and 12 rebounds.[^cite_note-194]

With the win, the Thunder, remaining undefeated, became the fourth defending champion to start the playoffs 8–0, as well as the first since the [2017 Cleveland Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Cleveland_Cavaliers_season). They were also the first defending champion since the [2019 Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Golden_State_Warriors_season) (who eventually lost to the [Toronto Raptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Toronto_Raptors_season) in the [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_NBA_Finals)) to advance to the conference finals.[^cite_note-195]

[Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) set a new NBA playoff record for blocks in a single game (12), finishing with 11 points and 15 rebounds. However, he struggled on the offensive end, going 5 of 17 from the field and alongside [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox); both stars shot a combined 0 of 12 from 3-point range. Minnesota built up a late 9-point lead, led by [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) and [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\)), with the latter coming off the bench in his return from an injury in the previous round. The Spurs staged a late rally to cut the Timberwolves' 9-point advantage to 2, but [Julian Champagnie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Champagnie)'s potential game-winning 3-pointer fell short at the rim, as the Wolves stole Game 1 and took a 1–0 series lead. Randle was the only player to reach over 20 points for the game, despite 12 other players finishing in double figures.[^cite_note-197]

San Antonio routed Minnesota, 133–95, to even the series at 1 apiece. After a close 1st quarter, the Spurs took a 24-point halftime lead and maintained control from there, with each of their starters scoring in double figures, including [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle)'s 21 points and [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama)'s 19 points and 15 rebounds. No Timberwolves player eclipsed 12 points, in an all-out defensive effort by San Antonio. This was their largest playoff loss in franchise history.[^cite_note-198]

&#x20;In just his second road playoff game, [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) took the spotlight with 39 points, 15 rebounds and 5 blocks, becoming only the fourth NBA player to achieve this feat in the playoffs. The Spurs began the game on a 14–1 run, seemingly taking early control before the Timberwolves made a late rally to keep it a close game, with the score being tied at halftime. After San Antonio took a 7-point lead headed into the fourth quarter, Minnesota made multiple comeback attempts but fell short each time, as the Spurs ended an 8-game losing streak at [Target Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Target_Center) and took a 2–1 series lead. [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\)) started the game coming off his knee injury, getting back into form with 32 points, 14 rebounds and 6 assists, but no other Timberwolves player reached over 20 points, as [Jaden McDaniels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaden_McDaniels) and [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) combined to shoot 8 of 34 from the field.[^cite_note-199]

Behind [Anthony Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Edwards_\(basketball\))' 36 points (16 in the fourth quarter), the Timberwolves avoided a 3–1 deficit and rallied past the Spurs to tie the series 2–2. Neither team led by double digits. After [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) got ejected in the second quarter due to a flagrant foul 2 on [Naz Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naz_Reid),[^cite_note-200] San Antonio took an 8-point lead entering the fourth quarter, but could not help but struggle against the surging Edwards. [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) and [Dylan Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Harper) each scored 24 points in a losing effort.[^cite_note-201]

[Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) returned with fury, finishing with 27 points (18 in the first quarter), 17 rebounds and five assists, to help push the Spurs pass the Timberwolves and take a 3–2 series lead. [Keldon Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keldon_Johnson) added 21 points, the best game in the playoffs thus far for the [Sixth Man of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Sixth_Man_of_the_Year), while [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) scored 18 and [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) had 17. Minnesota made it a game in the third quarter, but each time they would get within striking distance, San Antonio answered with rallies themselves.[^cite_note-202]

| --- |

&#x20;In a closeout game on the road, the Spurs never flinched and took a dominant 139–109 victory over the Timberwolves to advance to the Western Conference Finals for the first time since the [2016–17 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_San_Antonio_Spurs_season), and their second conference finals appearance since [Tim Duncan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Duncan)'s retirement. [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) led the way for San Antonio, finishing with 32 points on 11-for-16 shooting from the field and 5 of 7 from 3-point range, whereas [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) added 21 points and 9 assists. The Spurs set the tone early with a 29-point lead, as they never trailed throughout the game and led by as many as 37; despite a Minnesota rally late in the second quarter that cut the initial 29-point advantage to 12, San Antonio held off any other answer and put the game away in the second half. Despite committing only five turnovers, the Timberwolves were out-rebounded 60–29 and shot only 38% from the field, with their starters shooting a combined 18 of 59 from the field (30.5%). Notably, [Julius Randle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Randle) recorded just three points in a disastrous showing, while [Rudy Gobert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudy_Gobert) finished the game scoreless. Conversely, every San Antonio starter finished the game in double figures with a total of 101 points, just 8 points removed from Minnesota's entire game total.

This was the third straight Timberwolves season that ended in a non-competitive blowout. They trailed by 33 points at halftime in a 30-point loss at Oklahoma City in the Game 5 of the Western Conference finals [last year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_NBA_playoffs) and were down by 29 points at halftime to [Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023-24_Dallas_Mavericks_season) in the Western Conference finals in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NBA_playoffs) in a 21-point loss in Game 5.[^cite_note-203] The Timberwolves also lost a second round series for the first time in franchise history.

#### (3) New York Knicks vs. (4) Cleveland Cavaliers

&#x20;Nearly five months after a come-from-behind victory at [Madison Square Garden](75129.md) on [Christmas Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Christmas_games), the Knicks orchestrated another comeback win over the Cavaliers, spoiling [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell)'s Conference Finals debut. Cleveland built a 22-point lead in the fourth quarter, but New York outscored them 30–8 to finish regulation. [Sam Merrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Merrill) missed a game-winning three-pointer, sending the game to overtime.

[Landry Shamet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landry_Shamet), who came on strong down the stretch after Knicks coach [Mike Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Brown_\(basketball,_born_1970\)) benched [Josh Hart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Hart) in favor of him, scored the game-tying three-pointer to force overtime. [Jalen Brunson](43132078.md) led all New York scorers with 38 points, 17 of them in the fourth quarter and overtime. In a losing effort, Mitchell led Cleveland with 29 points, and [Evan Mobley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evan_Mobley) had a double-double with 15 points and 14 rebounds. [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden), playing in his first Conference Finals since [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NBA_playoffs#Western_Conference_finals), had 15 points, but was 1–8 on three-pointers and had 6 turnovers. He was also hunted on the defense, as Brunson scored 11 consecutive baskets during their comeback, with Harden as the primary defender. According to [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN), the Cavaliers had a 99.9% chance of winning with 7\:49 left in the fourth quarter. Since that point, the Knicks outscored them 44–11.[^cite_note-205] Their 22-point fourth quarter comeback after trailing 93–71 was the second-largest in NBA playoff history, after the [Los Angeles Clippers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Los_Angeles_Clippers_season) in Game 1 of their [first round series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_NBA_playoffs#\(4\)_Memphis_Grizzlies_vs._\(5\)_Los_Angeles_Clippers) against the [Memphis Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Memphis_Grizzlies_season), and the largest in Knicks franchise history. New York's winning streak for this playoffs was extended to eight games, dating back to Game 4 of the first-round series over the [Atlanta Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Atlanta_Hawks_season).[^cite_note-206]

| --- |

&#x20;The Knicks continued their offensive groove in the playoffs, winning their 11th playoff game in 33 days and extending their postseason winning streak to 10 games. The Cavaliers, who entered Game 3 with a 6–1 record at home, never led in the game and lost 3 consecutive games for the first time since December 2025. [Jalen Brunson](43132078.md) recorded 30 points, the fifth time this postseason he had eclipsed 30 points or more, while [Mikal Bridges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikal_Bridges) continued his spectacular performance on both ends of the floor, finishing with 22 points on 11–15 shooting, while collecting 3 steals and 2 blocks. [Landry Shamet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landry_Shamet) came off the bench and went 4–5 from beyond the arc, including 3 big shots in the 4th quarter to hold off Cleveland from making a rally. [Donovan Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donovan_Mitchell), [James Harden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Harden) and [Evan Mobley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evan_Mobley) combined for 66 points but shot a collective 5–23 from 3-point range and had 16 of 17 Cavaliers turnovers, most of them occurring in the third quarter. With this victory, the Knicks are now just one win away from advancing to the [NBA Finals](890793.md), looking to clinch their first appearance since [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_NBA_Finals).[^cite_note-208]

&#x20;The Knicks finished their dominant Eastern Conference playoff run with a 130–93 win over the Cavaliers to punch their ticket to the [NBA Finals](82427430.md) for the first time since [1999](17309687.md). They joined the [2017 Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Golden_State_Warriors_season), [2001 Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000%E2%80%9301_Los_Angeles_Lakers_season), [1999 Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%9399_San_Antonio_Spurs_season), and [1989 Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988%E2%80%9389_Los_Angeles_Lakers_season) as the only teams to win 11 consecutive games in a single-postseason. Starting with their Game 5 win against the [Atlanta Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Atlanta_Hawks_season) in Round 1, the Knicks have played the most dominant 10-game stretch in NBA history (regular season or playoffs), with a plus-206 point differential.[^cite_note-209] With the loss, despite making it to the Conference Finals, the Cavs finished their playoff run with an 8–10 record.[^cite_note-210]

#### (1) Oklahoma City Thunder vs. (2) San Antonio Spurs

[Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) propelled the Spurs to a thrilling double-overtime victory over the Thunder on the road, posting 41 points and 24 rebounds. He was the youngest player to record at least 40 points and 20 rebounds in a playoff game. [Alex Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Caruso) scored 31 points, one short of his career-high of 32 in 2019. [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md), who was recently named the [NBA MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Most_Valuable_Player) for the second straight year, finished with a double-double of 24 points and 12 assists. [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) (ankle) did not play, while [Jalen Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Williams) returned from a 6-game absence and contributed to 26 points. [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) blocked a potential Wembanyama game winner at the end of regulation to force overtime.

This was Oklahoma City's third double-overtime game of the season (including the playoffs), after their first two games against the [Houston Rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Houston_Rockets_season) and the [Indiana Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Indiana_Pacers_season), respectively, both of which ended in a Thunder win. The Thunder's nine-game postseason winning streak dating back to Game 7 of the [2025 NBA Finals](76692117.md) came to an end.[^cite_note-212]

&#x20;Behind a 30-point, 9-assist performance from MVP [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md), the Thunder evened up the series at 1 apiece with another tightly contested game against the Spurs. [Isaiah Hartenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaiah_Hartenstein) scored just 10 points, but had 13 rebounds, including 8 offensive, and [Alex Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Caruso) added 17 off the bench, as the team lost forward [Jalen Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Williams) in the first half due to a recurring hamstring strain. San Antonio were without All-Star [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) and rookie [Dylan Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Harper) themselves, the latter of whom came out of the game after an awkward fall in the third quarter. [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) led the Spurs with 25 points but had primary point guard duties with Fox and Harper out, and turned the ball over nine times, now committing 20 turnovers in the first two games. San Antonio received 22 points from [Devin Vassell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devin_Vassell) and [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) had 21 points, 17 rebounds, 6 assists, 4 blocks after his historic Game 1 performance.[^cite_note-213]

&#x20;26 points from [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) and 76 bench points from the Thunder was more than enough for Oklahoma City to take a 2–1 series lead in a 123–108 Game 3 win in San Antonio. The bench was led by [Jared McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jared_McCain) 24 points in 27 minutes. Backup big man [Jaylin Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaylin_Williams_\(basketball,_born_2002\)) added 18 points on five three-point shots and [Alex Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Caruso) added 15 on three 3-pointers. The Spurs, who started 15–0 to begin the game, had [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) and [Dylan Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Harper) activated for the game, but their shot from the field (43 percent) was worse than the Thunder's three-point shot rate (45 percent). [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) had 24 points but had just 4 rebounds and was outrebounded by [Isaiah Hartenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaiah_Hartenstein). [Devin Vassell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devin_Vassell) added 20 points and Fox scored 15 in his first game of the series.[^cite_note-214]

&#x20;Playing with a sense of urgency to avoid going down 3–1 in the series, the Spurs held the Thunder to their second-lowest postseason total, beating them 103–82 in Game 4. [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) had another effective all-around effort with 33 points (including a half-court shot as the clock expired before halftime), 8 rebounds, 5 assists and 3 blocks. Oklahoma City's bench was still effective, scoring 34 points, but well below their historic 76 points in Game 3. Overall for the team, it was a struggle as they shot just 33 percent and 18 percent from 3-point range, to go with 17 turnovers against 22 assists.[^cite_note-215]

&#x20;The Oklahoma City offense returned in Game 5 with a pivotal 127–114 win to go ahead in the series 3–2. The Thunder, who were held to 82 points in a Game 4 loss two days earlier, had 82 points less than four minutes into the third quarter. [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) had 32 points on 16–17 shooting from the free throw line. [Alex Caruso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Caruso) led the bench with another big performance with 22 points and 4-for-8 from the 3 point line. [Isaiah Hartenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaiah_Hartenstein) led the team in plus-minus (plus-24) with a double-double effort of 12 points on 6–8 shooting and 15 rebounds, while his backcourt teammate [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) had his best game of the series with 16 points on 6–9 shooting and 11 rebounds. [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) scored 24 points for San Antonio, who got 22 points from [Julian Champagnie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Champagnie) and 20 from [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) — who was held to 4-of-15 shooting in his worst game of the series. The Spurs missed 29 of their 41 3-point tries. There was 70 free-throws attempted in the game. Postgame, the referees were a topic of discussion for the game and series, who after letting the two teams play in Game 1, have called the series tight since.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] Many also accused the Thunder, specifically NBA's MVP Gilgeous-Alexander, of [flopping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flop_\(basketball\)).[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219]

&#x20;The Spurs forced the 160th Game 7 in NBA history and the first in the Western Conference Finals since [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NBA_playoffs), with a 118–91 rout in Game 6. [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) led the charge with 28 points, 10 rebounds and three blocks, while rookie [Dylan Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Harper) scored 18 points off the bench and [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) finished with 17 points and 9 assists. San Antonio effectively iced the game in the third quarter with a 20–0 run. [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) was limited to 15 points on 6-for-18 shooting. [Jalen Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Williams) returned for Oklahoma City but had just one point in a 10-minute role off the bench.[^cite_note-220]

Game 7 was the Spurs' first since losing to the [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Denver_Nuggets_season) in the first round of the [2019 playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_NBA_playoffs#\(2\)_Denver_Nuggets_vs._\(7\)_San_Antonio_Spurs); no players on the 2019 team are still on San Antonio's active roster.[^cite_note-221] The last time the Thunder played in a Game 7, they clinched the NBA championship [last season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_Oklahoma_City_Thunder_season).

Further information: [List of NBA game sevens](15664762.md)

Before the game, it was announced that [Jalen Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalen_Williams) (hamstring) and [Ajay Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajay_Mitchell) (right soleus) would not play.

The Spurs dethroned the defending champion Thunder in a 111–103 victory to advance to their first NBA Finals since [2014](39572289.md). The score was Spurs 80, Thunder 77 entering the fourth quarter, as the game was a back-and-forth contest where the Spurs led by as many as 14 in the first half and then by as many as 11 in the third, only to see the Thunder come roaring back both times. The Spurs pulled ahead by 12 again in midway through the fourth, but the Thunder stormed back again. There were many huge plays in the game, but perhaps the biggest came midway through the fourth during a Thunder momentum swing run, when San Antonio’s back-up big [Luke Kornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Kornet) blocked [Isaiah Hartenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaiah_Hartenstein) at the rim, denying a fast-break score that would have gotten the Thunder within four.[^cite_note-222]

Despite foul trouble in the fourth, [Victor Wembanyama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Wembanyama) scored 22 points and had 7 rebounds in 42 minutes played. [Julian Champagnie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Champagnie) got 18 of his 20 off of 3-pointers and [Stephon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephon_Castle) had a 16 points, 6 rebounds, and 6 assists (against 6 turnovers). [De'Aaron Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De'Aaron_Fox) chipped in with 15 points and rookie guard [Dylan Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Harper) had 12 points and 7 rebounds. [Shai Gilgeous-Alexander](54516984.md) had his best game of the series, scoring 35 points on 12–21 shooting and 9 assists. But he received little help outside of [Cason Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cason_Wallace)'s 17 points on 6–10 shooting. [All-NBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-NBA_Team) Third Team forward/center [Chet Holmgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chet_Holmgren) had just 4 points and only took two shots, making his 4 points the second-lowest scoring output by an All-NBA player in a Game 7 in NBA history.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]

Over the course of the season, San Antonio gave Oklahoma City eight of their 22 losses.[^cite_note-225] The Thunder finished the regular season 64–18 and the playoffs 11–4, or 75–22 overall, going 1–4 against the Spurs in the regular season and 3–4 against the Spurs in the playoffs, while going 63–14 in the regular season and 8–0 in the playoffs against the rest of the league, or 71–14 overall against the rest of the league versus a record of 4–8 overall against the Spurs.

## Media coverage

This was the first postseason of new 11-year U.S. media deals with the [ESPN family of networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_on_ESPN), [NBC Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_on_NBC) and [Amazon Prime Video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_on_Prime_Video).[^cite_note-smw_unsealed_details-229] These deals marked the first time that all first-round playoff games in the U.S. were exclusive national games, and regional broadcasters could no longer produce their own local feeds.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] The agreement with Prime Video also resulted in this year being the first time that selected playoffs games would only be available in the U.S on a [streaming service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_television).[^cite_note-sn2026-232]

ESPN and [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_on_ABC) had approximately 18 games in the first two rounds, with those games also streaming on [ESPN DTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_\(streaming_service\)). NBC Sports produced at least 15 games in the first round, and at least seven games in the second round; at least seven first round and four second round games aired over-the-air on NBC, with the remaining games on [Peacock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacock_\(streaming_service\)) and [NBCSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCSN). Peacock also simulcast all games that air on the main NBC broadcast network. Amazon Prime Video streamed at least nine first-round playoff games and at least five second-round playoff games.[^cite_note-smw_unsealed_details-229][^cite_note-\:0-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-235]

During the first two rounds, Saturday afternoon games were split among Prime Video and NBC Sports. The rest of the first week generally resembled [coverage during the regular season](79260681.md#Television) with ABC airing games on Saturday nights and Sunday afternoons, NBC Sports producing games on Sunday through Tuesday nights, ESPN on Wednesdays, and Prime Video on Thursdays and Fridays. The broadcasting assignments of the second week were then adjusted to ensure each broadcaster met their required number of games in the round.[^cite_note-schedule-236] Game 7 of the Cleveland Cavaliers–Detroit Pistons conference semifinal was thus assigned to Prime Video, marking the first time an NBA Game 7 was only available in the U.S on a [streaming service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_television).[^cite_note-sn2026-232]

As per the alternating rotation, NBC and Peacock had the Western Conference Finals this season, while ABC and ESPN had the Eastern Conference Finals.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] ABC will have the NBA Finals for the 24th straight year, along with it also being streamed on ESPN DTC.[^cite_note-smw_unsealed_details-229]

In Canada, the home market of the Toronto Raptors, English national broadcast rights were split approximately equally between the [Sportsnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportsnet) and [TSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sports_Network) groups of channels. Under those rights, the two broadcasters were allowed to produce separate Canadian feeds for all games involving the Raptors regardless of round or U.S. broadcaster. Sportsnet and TSN simulcast the U.S.-based feed for all other series.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240]

All games airing on Sportsnet are also simulcast on Sportsnet+.[^cite_note-241]

[Awful Announcing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Awful_Announcing) criticized NBC Sports' decision during both the Monday and Tuesday of first week of the conference semifinals to have overlapping games at 8 and 9\:30 p.m. ET, with one on over-the-air NBC and the other on NBCSN (with both available on Peacock), instead of the standard weeknight doubleheaders at 7 and 9\:30 p.m. ET, when on May 4 (Monday), Game 1 of the Philadelphia 76ers–New York Knicks series on NBC became a 137–98 Knicks blowout while Game 1 of the Minnesota Timberwolves–San Antonio Spurs series on NBCSN ended with a 104–102 Timberwolves win at the buzzer.[^cite_note-242]

### Viewership

See also: [2026 NBA Finals § Viewership](82427430.md#Viewership)

Playoff viewership during the first round on April 26 indicated it was their highest since [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_NBA_playoffs), with an average of 3.84 million viewers per game across ESPN, ABC, NBC, Peacock and Prime Video. The increase in national viewership was attributed to the elimination of first-round regional broadcasts, as well as over-the-air broadcaster NBC taking over a portion of the coverage that was previously held by cable channel [TNT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_on_TNT).[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244]

Game 7 of Spurs–Thunder Western Conference Finals (15.9 million viewers) on NBC was the highest rated non-NBA Finals playoff game since the Thunder–Warriors on TNT in [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NBA_playoffs) (16.0 million).[^cite_note-245]

The NBA Finals was the most-watched since [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_NBA_Finals), with an average of 20.6 million viewers.[^cite_note-246]

## Sponsorship

For the fifth straight year, the playoffs is officially known as the "2026 NBA Playoffs presented by Google". During the expanded sponsorship agreement with [Google](1092923.md) where it expanded the sponsorship from the "Google Pixel" brand in favor of the general Google branding, this sponsorship provides the logo branding inside the venues and in official digital properties on-court, as well as commercial inventory during ESPN, ABC, NBC and Amazon Prime Video telecasts of the playoff games.[^cite_note-256] In Canada, the NBA Playoffs are presented by MyRocky, a Canadian telehealth partner.[^cite_note-257] NewPath Sports & Entertainment Inc. secured the Canadian rights.
